Platts European Diesel Daily Market Analysis
European diesel prices surge on Middle East risks
Russian tanker departs for US
The European diesel complex extended its gains Oct. 23, amid bullish market sentiment over risks to oil operations with escalations in the Middle East.
Platts assessed the front-month ICE low sulfur gasoil futures contract up $45/metric ton to $1,492.25/mt Oct.23, the highest level seen in the market since Oct. 8.
Oil prices have climbed as renewed Houthi attacks raise risks to Saudi Arabia's oil infrastructure and the export routes the kingdom has increasingly relied on amid disruption to shipping through the Strait of Hormuz.
Saudi crude continues to reach international markets via Yanbu, the Suez Canal and Egypt's SUMED pipeline, but the latest attacks have put the market on edge over both production infrastructure and alternative export routes out of the Middle East.
"The threat is no longer one route or one front: Iran, the Houthis in Yemen and militias in Iraq can all put Middle East oil infrastructure at risk," according to the latest crude oil market outlook from S&P Global Energy CERA.
Moreover, the first diesel tanker from Russia to the US since 2022 departed Oct. 23, but has done little to alleviate pressure on the global diesel market.
The Malta-flagged medium-sized tanker Agioi Fanendes is heading to New York carrying Russian diesel, according to S&P Global Energy Commodities at Sea data, with the Kremlin

expecting deliveries to continue "on a regular basis." The Kremlin's spokesperson, Dmitry Peskov, said Oct. 23 that
the first tanker carrying Russian diesel was sent to the US. "The first tanker has, in fact, set off toward its destinations
-- to its buyers -- and [subsequent shipments] will continue on a regular basis," Peskov told journalists, according to state-owned news agency TASS. He did not clarify the name of the vessel sent to the US or who the buyers of the delivery are.
The Agioi Fanendes tanker left Russia's port of Primorsk Oct. 22 and is expected to reach New York around Nov. 6-7, according to CAS data. It is unclear who the buyer and the seller of the vessel might be.
This comes after the US Treasury Department's Office of Foreign Assets Control granted Oct. 9, a sanctions waiver to Russia allowing the sale, delivery and import of Russian-origin diesel fuel, including into the US.
However, market participants are doubtful that Russian exports to the US will bring any material change to product availability or prices in Europe.
The delivery of these diesel volumes to the US "would only be a displacement of volume from Brazil," according to a Europebased diesel trader. "It is a logistical change regarding where the product goes, rather than an increase in flows. We would have to see vastly more flows for it to [affect] the European market."
The trader added that "the product was not coming to Europe anyway," highlighting the perceived minimal impact of the news.
Platts is part of S&P Global Energy. -- Sophia Aung
Marine Fuel
Q3 oil bunker sales rise in Antwerp, Rotterdam while LNG and biofuels falter
LNG pricing surge triggers fuel switching
Biofuel sales drop as pooling costs decline
Antwerp on track to overtake Rotterdam
Bunker fuel sales strengthened at Antwerp-Bruges and Rotterdam in the third quarter, according to data from the port authorities, with stronger demand for oil-based fuels offsetting lower deliveries of LNG and biofuels.
The development came as a surge in LNG price made the fuel less competitive, while shipping companies with conventional ships could meet compliance requirements via pooling arrangements at lower costs than using biofuels.
Total bunker sales at Antwerp-Bruges rose 5.3% quarter on quarter to 2.158 million metric tons in Q3, the highest in seven quarters, the Belgian port complex's authority said in an Oct. 22 report.
Very low sulfur fuel oil sales rose 25.4% from Q2 to 674,713 mt, while high sulfur fuel oil sales increased 7.3% to 630,706 mt. Ultra low sulfur fuel oil sales slipped 3.2% to 159,663 mt and gasoil sales fell 4.7% to 348,957 mt.

23

©2026 S&P Global Inc. Any unauthorized use of this copyrighted material constitutes infringement of our intellectual property and will subject you to possible legal action.

Platts Oilgram Price Report

October 23, 2026

The Port of Rotterdam recorded 1.95 million mt in total bunker sales in Q3, up 3.4% from the second quarter, supported by increases across its main fossil fuel oil grades.
ULSFO sales climbed 34.2% quarter on quarter to 165,318 mt, VLSFO sales rose 20.6% to 427,347 mt and HSFO sales increased 7.1% to 754,853 mt. Marine gasoil sales rose 9.5% to 291,852 mt, while marine diesel oil sales declined 11.8% to 57,155 mt.
LNG, biofuel bunker sales drop Delivered bunker prices for VLSFO in Rotterdam fell to $16.146/
gigajoule in Q3 from $16.891/GJ in Q2, while those for LNG rose to $22.249/GJ from $16.857/GJ, according to Platts assessments from S&P Global Energy.
Sources said the price swings and uncompetitive spreads to alternatives created some demand destruction in the LNG bunker spot market, with many noting comparisons to the landscape in 2022 following Russia's invasion of Ukraine.
"It is not a good market for buyers right now, especially those on the spot market...there is more fuel switching going on now," an Atlantic-based trader said.
Antwerp-Bruges' LNG sales slipped 2.6% from Q2 to 74,499 mt in Q3, while Rotterdam reported a steeper 21.1% decline to 215,420 cubic meters. Both are were the lowest quarterly figures so far in 2026.
In contrast, bio-LNG bunker sales more than tripled quarter on quarter to 23,613 cubic meters from 7,506 cubic meters, exceeding the previous record of 15,260 cubic meters in Q1.
The Zero Emission Maritime Buyers Alliance said Oct. 22 that it had concluded the use of waste-based biomethane under the alliance's first shipping tender. ZEMBA members, including IKEA and Amazon, transported containers on Hapag-Lloyd's ships that consumed 20,000 mt of bio-LNG in 2025-2026.
But Rotterdam's total biobunker sales fell 37.6% from Q2 to 154,234 mt, led by a 56.3% drop in bio-VLSFO to 63,184 mt. Bio-HSFO fell 57.8% to 12,728 mt, while bio-MGO slipped 4.3% to 33,083 mt.
Antwerp-Bruges' biofuel sales halved quarter on quarter to 13,459 mt, representing just 0.6% of total bunker demand.
Weaker demand came as ship operators could comply with FuelEU Maritime by joining pools at competitive prices, with the OceanScore Pool-Price Index for compliance units sliding from 131.75/mt of CO2-equivalent in July to 118.90/mtCO2e in August, 108.60/mtCO2e in September and 95.80/mtCO2e in October.
The average cost of switching from VLSFO to B30 blend with 30% used cooking oil methyl ester and 70% VLSFO in Rotterdam was $442.44/mtCO2e in Q3, according to Platts FuelEU CO2 abatement assessments that did not take into account the Emissions Trading System.
Ports diverge The quarter-on-quarter recovery masked divergent annual
trends that have put Antwerp on track to become the largest European marine bunker port this year.
Bunker deliveries at Antwerp-Bruges amounted to 6.308

million mt in January-September, up 3.8% from 6.075 million mt a year earlier. Combined fossil and bio-blended bunker sales at Rotterdam fell 23.9% over the same period to 5.630 million mt from 7.396 million mt.
The Netherlands has implemented the EU's Renewable Energy Directive III since January, prompting Rotterdam-based suppliers to include compliance costs in their sale prices, while the national transposition in Belgium continues to face delays.
The premium of delivered VLSFO bunker prices in Rotterdam to Antwerp was $21.554/mt in Q3 2026, compared with 15.60 cents/mt in Q4 2025, according to Platts assessments.

Higher freight pressures Americas-Europe HSFO arbitrage despite record premiums
High freight costs limit HSFO shipments from the Americas Europe HSFO premium hits record $103.25/mt over USGC

The high sulfur fuel oil arbitrage window from the Americas to Europe has been pressured by acutely elevated dirty tanker freight rates, despite record premiums in Europe over the US Gulf Coast.
The premium of physical 3.5% Rotterdam barges over RMG 380 USGC 3.5% barges was $103.25/mt, Oct. 21 the highest premium on record, according to data from Platts, part of S&P Global Energy.
However, dirty USGC-UKC aframax freight rates were last assessed at $150.22/mt, Oct. 22, exceeding the arbitrage premium and up from $39.29/mt a year prior, Platts data showed.
Within the European market, participants anticipate lower volumes of HSFO entering from South America and the US amid this challenging arbitrage dynamic, which has supported European differentials.

"Arb from Americas is dynamic, differentials in Europe are high, but so is freight, not that many cargoes are coming to Europe," said a European trader source.
A second trader said that higher freight rates are causing some resupply concerns within the market.
Paper markets underscored this outlook, with Platts assessing the front-month FOB Rotterdam barge HSFO crack against dated Brent at minus $8.68/b on Oct. 22, up from minus $11.60/b at the beginning of the month.
Overall bunkering demand, however, has been subdued as the higher flat prices weigh on end user buying appetite, contributing to a balanced market across the European HSFO market.
US Gulf Coast HSFO barge pricing has noticeably declined in the latter half of October, driven by increased selling interest amid ample regional supply.
The decrease was driven by competitive sell-side interest observed over multiple days during the Platts Market on Close assessment process, with a traded offer reaching as low as a $1/b premium to the Month 1 Nov. USGC HSFO swap on Oct. 20.
The increased MOC selling activity comes amid greater HSFO availability in the region, due in part to higher imports of Venezuelan crude oil into the US Gulf Coast.
The additional Venezuelan crude being processed in the USGC is yielding more HSFO than the region's cokers can absorb, pushing excess volumes into the bunkers pool.
The ample supply on the US Gulf Coast, coupled with higher freight rates that make arbitrage barrels to Europe unviable, has led to a softening of the HSFO pricing environment in the USGC.
"The arb should be opening, but with [high freight costs], that spread has a ways to go," a US-based fuel oil source said.
Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, assessed the Oct. 22 USGC HSFO bulk value price at $84.30/b, down $6.29/b, or 6.9 percent, from a recent multi-month high of $90.59/b on Oct. 16.
-- Tommy Petrou, Humza Mirza, Iman Rezig
SINGAPORE DATA: Heavy distillate stocks rebound 4.5% to four-week high
Imports rise 75% week over week
Middle Eastern inflows nearly double
Exports from Singapore up 226%
Singapore's commercial stockpiles of heavy distillates rebounded 4.5% to 18.858 million barrels in the week ended Oct. 21, following six consecutive weeks of inventory draws, Enterprise Singapore data showed Oct. 22.
However, stocks were 18.1% lower in the week ended Oct. 21 than in the corresponding week a year earlier, the data also showed.
Weekly residual fuel stocks in Singapore have averaged 20.709 million barrels so far in 2026, compared with a weekly average of 22.8 million barrels in 2025 and 19.7 million barrels in 2024, according to Enterprise Singapore data.

The weekly build in Singapore's inventories was partly driven by a 74.9% week-over-week increase in imports, which totaled 965,469 mt in the week ended Oct. 21.
About 44.3% of the imported volume came from within Asia, with Indonesia and India supplying 77,144 mt and 63,765 mt of heavy distillates, respectively. Arrivals from Thailand jumped to 116,665 mt from 31,926 mt in the preceding week.
Imports from the Middle East -- the UAE, Syria and Saudi Arabia -- increased 91.1% week over week to 297,519 mt in the week ended Oct. 21.
Singapore also saw 64,920 mt of arrivals from the Netherlands, after receiving no European inflows for two weeks.
Meanwhile, fuel oil exports from Singapore more than tripled from the preceding week to 440,316 mt in the week ended Oct. 21.
Of the total, 208,378 mt was bound for China, up from 84,062 mt the week before.
Singapore's inventory data covers only stocks at onshore terminals. Enterprise Singapore describes heavy distillates as "residues," which include cracked and straight-run fuel oil and low-sulfur waxy residue.
In the downstream market, spot trading discussions at the Singapore bunker hub were steady in the week to Oct. 22, with healthy supply and demand noted for November, according to trade sources.
Firmer cargo prices on Oct. 22 raised concerns that premiums could rise, prompting last-minute buying for October deliveries, two Singapore-based traders said.
Limited barging slots for the rest of October kept premiums elevated, with mostly pocket slots available. Additional offers for October deliveries hinged on vessel schedules, with delays potentially freeing up more supply, a Singapore-based trader said.
Platts assessed the Singapore-delivered 0.5%S marine fuel bunker premium over benchmark FOB Singapore marine fuel 0.5%S cargo prices at $86.02/mt on Oct. 22, up 38% week over week and the highest in about two months.
Platts assessed the Singapore-delivered 380 CST HSFO bunker premium over FOB Singapore 380 CST HSFO cargo prices at $95.97/mt, up 11% week over week and the highest in about seven months.
Premiums for low-sulfur fuel oil ex-wharf cargoes were steady to slightly firmer Oct. 22, with first-half November loadings heard traded from the low $40s/mt to $45/mt, according to trade sources.
-- Iris Leung, Nur syamieza Ahmad zubir

Platts European Fuel Oil Daily Market Analysis
ARA fuel oil stocks decrease 0.9% to 930,000 mt VLSFO exports rise on Americas, Asia premiums High freight costs limit HSFO imports from Americas

Very low sulfur fuel oil product moved out of Europe Oct. 23, while freight rates weighed on HSFO imports and Amsterdam-Rotterdam-Antwerp stocks slipped week over week.
In the Northwest European fuel oil market, ARA stocks fell 0.9% to 930,000 metric tons in the week to Oct. 22, according to Insights Global data, after building the previous week.
In the European VLSFO market, higher flat prices weighed on end-user buying appetite. However, elevated premiums in the Americas and Asian markets have led to volume flowing out of Europe on arbitrage routes, thereby supporting prices.
Overall, however, there was ample availability of blend stocks such as low sulfur straight run, where a well-supplied market had pushed blenders to sell straight run product into the VLSFO bunkering pool, participants said.
"The market is swimming in LSSR," said a trader. In other news, Cyprus utility demand had helped support the European LSFO market, with a second trader noting movement of product from Northwest Europe into the Mediterranean to supply utility demand. In the high sulfur fuel oil market, participants described fundamentals as "balanced." However, there were some resupply concerns centered around the closed arbitrage window from the Americas region. "The arb from the Americas is dynamic, differentials in Europe are high, but so is freight. Not that many cargoes are coming to Europe," said another European trader. Another source said that "[HSFO] blending is more challenging" in the Mediterranean. There were no trades in the MOC for fuel oil cargoes. Platts is part of S&P Global Energy.

Backwardation of the Asian naphtha market steepened amid mounting tensions in the Middle East Oct. 23.

In the derivatives market, brokers pegged the front-month November-December Mean of Platts Japan swaps time spread at $43/mt in midafternoon Asian trading Oct. 22, up $4.75/mt from the previous close.

a premium of about $17-$17.25/mt to MOPJ naphtha assessments, CFR, with 45-day pricing prior to delivery. They have also procured H2 December deliveries via a tender that closed Oct. 14.

Gas Liquids
North American LPG prices hit multiyear highs amid strong domestic, export demand
US propane exports above 2 million b/d on average in October
US propane inventories hit lowest since August
Edmonton butane prices reach all-time Platts high
North American LPG prices reached multiyear highs in late October, driven by robust domestic demand across the continent

and strong exports to regions such as the Far East. Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, assessed US Gulf Coast
propane prices at $1.0925/gallon on Oct. 22. This marked one of the highest prices since September 2022, while the propane-to-crude ratio was about 46%, on par with a year ago.
The Mont Belvieu propane-to-crude ratio at Energy Transfer consolidated at 47% after rising steadily over the past month. The Enterprise propane ratio was 46%. Both ratios are now in line with 2025.
The rising Gulf Coast propane price comes as propane exports out of the US have held above 2 million barrels/day over the last four weeks, according to S&P Global Commodities at Sea shipping data.
In October, Japan was the largest importer, accounting for about 27% of all US propane loadings, with China following at 17%.
This steady export trend was part of the reason US propane and propylene inventories fell below 106 million barrels for the first time since early August during the week ended Oct. 16. Stocks tightened by 500,000 barrels to 105.846 million barrels, their lowest level since early August, according to US Energy Information Administration data released Oct. 21 for the week ended Oct. 16.
The US Gulf Coast propane price has also affected propane prices further north in Canada. Platts assessed October-delivery propane in Edmonton up 1.375 cent/gal to 96.375 cents/gal, according to Platts data. November deliveries also gained, rising 1.875 cent/gal to 96.125 cents/gal, narrowing the prompt-toforward spread.
Stronger domestic, export butane demand North American butane prices, meanwhile, have remained
bullish, particularly since the start of the US shift to wintergrade gasoline, which was pushed forward two weeks to Sept. 1. Butane is typically used as a blendstock in higher-RVP gasoline blending.
Platts assessed butane prices at the Enterprise terminal at $1.785/gal on Oct. 22, marking one of the highest levels since March 2022. The Conway-Enterprise butane price spread, meanwhile, was over 10 cents, compared with less than a cent a year ago.
The Conway-Enterprise price disparity has shown that blenders in the New York Harbor and smaller regional markets are facing very thin margins due to high rail and truck delivery costs, S&P Global Energy CERA analysts said in their Oct. 22 US NGLs report.
That's been part of the reason butane demand in Canada is currently up for use in the butane-tight US Midwest region, as reflected in Western Canadian prices.
Edmonton field-grade butane held firm relative to crude, remaining at 72.50% of the October WTI CMA for prompt deliveries, while November was also steady at 67.50% of the WTI CMA. The absolute differential, however, widened by 1.27 cents/ gal to $1.66/gal on Oct. 22.

Much of the US supply moving into Europe was already committed under contract, a trader said, leaving limited flexibility in the spot market.
Europe needed to remain competitive with Asian destinations to attract additional propane, keeping arbitrage economics central to the availability of incremental cargoes.
Inland, the effects of low Rhine water levels continued to restrict deliveries into Germany. Conditions had improved, but barge movements had yet to recover fully, limiting the volume of propane reaching inland consumers.
"We're still recovering from the low water level," the trader said. "We're still shipping low volumes on the barges."
Barges continued to operate below half their normal loading capacity, restricting inland transportation and weighing on demand through the barge market.

Demand could be "more satisfied by FCA probably than by barge," the trader added. Refinery activity and the use of propane in gas supply applications were also cited as potential constraints on availability, although the participant noted that substitution into the gas stream was not a like-for-like process. The market consequently remained shaped by both access to incremental seaborne supply and the ability to distribute existing volumes inland, with subdued barge activity reflecting persistent logistical constraints. Platts European Butane Daily Commentary
Refiners retain butane for winter blending
Supply tightens amid import disruptions
CIF large cargo prices jump $54.75/mt

Northwest European butane availability remained tight Oct. 23 as refinery retention for winter gasoline blending restricted merchant supply, while limited import replenishment left buyers with few alternatives for prompt coverage.
Winter gasoline specifications continued to underpin demand, allowing greater butane inclusion in the blending pool.
Refiners prioritized internal requirements, reducing volumes available for spot sale.
However, the reduced competitiveness of butane as a blendstock tempered buying interest, leaving demand steady rather than indicating a broader increase in consumption.
A trader identified winter-specification gasoline demand as the main influence on the butane market and described the product as expected to be "priced out of the petrochemical pool."
Petrochemical consumption consequently offered limited additional support, with butane struggling to compete with alternative feedstocks.
The market remained largely dependent on gasoline blending requirements and the availability of merchant barrels.
Meanwhile, supply relief remained limited. Disruptions to Middle Eastern exports constrained global availability, particularly for butane carried in split LPG cargoes, while limited US butane availability ahead of winter restricted opportunities for transatlantic replenishment.
Eastern European demand, particularly from Poland, continued to draw on Northwest European supply, intensifying competition for available volumes.
Low Rhine water levels also complicated inland distribution, limiting barge loadings and making delivery requirements harder to meet.
With refiners retaining material and imports providing little relief, prompt availability remained the primary constraint despite selective buying and limited petrochemical demand.
In the butane CIF NWE large cargo market, one bid was heard. Stasco was seen bidding a 12,000 metric ton cargo meeting Lyondell's specifications, for a Rotterdam Nov. 2-6 delivery, priced at 116% of naphtha CIF NWE November. It was deemed competitive and set the assessment.
Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, assessed the CIF large cargo market in outright terms at $1,091/mt, up $54.75/mt day over day and up 1 percentage point to Platts CIF NWE naphtha at 112%.
In the absence of indications in the Platts Market on Close assessment process, the butane coaster market was assessed on trader sentiment.
Platts assessed the FOB Seagoing butane coaster market at $1,285/mt, up $65/mt day over day and 1.25 percentage point above Platts CIF NWE naphtha at 132%.
Platts assessed the CIF seagoing market at $1,188/mt, up $49/mt day over day and stable as a percentage to Platts CIF NWE naphtha at 122%.
This put the FOB coasters at a $97/mt premium over the CIF coaster market.

Platts Western Mediterranean LPG Daily Commentary
Butane outperforms propane amid tight supply
US exports face Asia competition for cargoes
Mild weather delays European heating demand
The West Mediterranean LPG market ended the week Oct. 23 with tight global supply, stretched shipping and a reshuffling of long-haul flows outweighing lackluster regional demand
Butane stayed the stronger grade, while propane traded in a more balanced market as heating demand was slow to arrive.
Middle East supply disruptions kept the wider LPG balance tight. Disruptions to Middle East supply and shipping lifted demand for barrels sourced outside the region, and that pull was felt most in butane.
US exports remained the key swing source, but the transatlantic arbitrage into the Mediterranean faced stiff competition. The Middle East conflict widened the US-to-Asia arbitrage window, drawing Gulf Coast cargoes east and thinning the pool of spot US barrels available to Mediterranean and North African buyers.
Elevated freight also weighed on the northern route. Coaster movements from Amsterdam-Rotterdam-Antwerp and the UK into Morocco remained costly, which kept North African demand anchored to Mediterranean supply.
End-user demand offered limited support. Mild October weather across Europe delayed the seasonal pickup in heating and cylinder offtake.
Petrochemical appetite also remained subdued, with European olefins producers contending with weak underlying demand and ample supply.
Crackers, therefore, had little incentive to step in for Mediterranean LPG. North African import demand, chiefly Moroccan butane, was steady but largely covered through term and contract volumes, which limited spot inquiries.
Participants expected butane to remain well supported into November as blending and cylinder demand build and as Middle East disruptions keep competition for Atlantic Basin barrels intense.
Propane's direction was seen hinging on the arrival of colder weather and on whether US cargoes return to the Mediterranean as the eastbound arbitrage narrows.
Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, assessed the West Mediterranean FOB butane market at $1,246/metric ton, up $60/ mt day over day and up 1 percentage point versus naphtha at 128%, or at a $39/mt discount to FOB Northwest Europe coasters.
Platts assessed coaster freight from Lavera to Mohammedia at $66/mt, steady day over day, based on market information received throughout the day.
The CIF Mediterranean butane large cargo market was assessed at $1,121/mt, or 115.25% of naphtha.
Platts assessed the West Mediterranean FOB propane market at $954.50/mt, up $24.50/mt day over day.

The CIF Med propane large cargo market was assessed at $849.75/mt, or at a $17.75/mt premium to CIF Northwest Europe large cargoes.
In Morocco, Platts calculated the CIF very large cargo freight forward at $987.50/mt for delivery 45-60 days forward, up $8/mt.
Platts is part of S&P Global Energy. -- Baran Serdaroglu
Tankers
Hormuz traffic rises while Bab al-Mandab transits slow
Hormuz sees 23 vessels, up from 19
Bab al-Mandab drops to 27, lowest since Sep
Three tankers attacked near Iraq and Oman
Observed and inferred vessel movements through the Strait of Hormuz rose to 23 on Oct. 22, from a revised 19 a day earlier, while Bab al-Mandab Strait crossings fell to 27 from 35, S&P Global Commodities at Sea said in an Oct. 23 report.
Hormuz traffic comprised five bulk carriers, four product tankers, four VLCCs, one LPG carrier, one container ship, two cargo vessels and six vessels from other segments, CAS said.
Traffic was broadly balanced by direction, with 11 inbound and 12 outbound movements. Nine vessels were assessed as Iranlinked, while the remaining 14 were classified as compliant, CAS data showed.
Traffic across the US naval blockade line fell to 44 transits on Oct. 22 from 65 a day earlier, the lowest daily count since Oct. 12. Tanker movements across the line dropped to 25, also the lowest since Oct. 12.
"While the decline may reflect softer identifiable traffic, it could also indicate a greater share of activity being conducted without AIS visibility across the Arabian Sea," CAS analysts said. Total movements comprised 24 inbound passages toward the Gulf of Oman and 20 outbound passages.
As of Oct. 23, weekly transits were 114 outbound and 127 inbound, compared with 25 outbound and 129 inbound the previous week, according to UK Maritime Trade Operations. Overall activity was about 70% below pre-conflict levels. AISobserved transits rose to 47 from 42 the previous week, while US-reported facilitated transits were broadly unchanged, UKMTO said.
Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, assessed the freight rate for carrying 270,000 mt of crude from the Persian Gulf to China at $234.93/mt on Oct. 23, down $2.08/mt on the day. The rate for carrying 65,000 mt of refined products from the Gulf to the UKContinent was unchanged at $233.85/mt.
At the Bab al-Mandab Strait, total traffic fell to 27 transits, the lowest daily level since Sept. 16, CAS said. The count included nine tankers, down from 11 a day earlier and the lowest tanker tally since Oct. 11. There were 11 inbound and 16 outbound transits, CAS data showed.

Platts East of Suez Clean Tanker Daily Commentary
MR tankers reposition to Asia for higher pay
LR rates expected to drop on weak demand
East of Suez Medium Range, or MRs were mostly steady Oct. 23 with several tankers ballasting from Africa to Southeast Asia in their quest for higher earnings, market participants said.
More than a dozen MRs are moving towards Singapore, after discharging their cargoes in Africa, said a chartering executive with a global commodities trading company.
The returns are higher for loading cargoes in Southeast Asia for delivery Down Under, compared with Oman or West Coast India loadings for East and South Africa delivery, said a source with a clean oil tanker owner.
The daily scrubber-fitted MR earnings on the benchmark Singapore-Australia are close to $67,000-$77,000, basis round voyage, assuming high sulfur fuel oil bunker prices of around $875/mt, according to the estimates of brokers.
The corresponding daily earnings on the West Coast India/ Oman to East Africa route are at around $57,000-$58,000, assuming HSFO bunker prices of around $730/mt, the brokers said. For older MRs, these earnings are even lower, at around $54,000, the brokers added.
The Great Thita, which was ballasted from Durban, was placed on subjects at $4.85 million by Total for early November diesel loading on the Quanzhou-South Africa route, brokers said. The Seaways Lily, which ballasted from South Africa, was snapped up for an end-October short cross-harbor voyage in Singapore at $1.075 million by Petco, brokers added.
However, the demand for MRs has picked up in the Middle East and several among them, such as, the Virgo, the Torm Torino, the Torm Sublime, the T Jungfrau, the Imperial and the Largo Atom have been placed on subjects but no details were available, sources said. Lesser supply amid strong demand can support freight rates in the near term, they said.
No LR1 cargoes for November loading were heard and with plenty of vessels available for employment, rates are expected to fall for this segment, a second LR broker said. Outbound cargo movement from the Strait of Hormuz could be hampered, leading to a lack of cargoes on the market, a LR broker said.
In a recent fixture, a LR2, the Stresa, was placed on subjects

at w485 by Reliance for Oct. 28 loading on the Sikka-Australia route, sources said. A LR broker said that because this route sees a premium over Sikka-Japan and features a prompt loading date, next-done transactions could fall below this level.
A LR1, the Gem No. 3 was placed on subjects at w500 by Trafigura, for Nov. 2 diesel loading on the Duqm-East Africa route, sources said. The Hafnia Excellence was placed on subjects at w525 by OQ, for Nov. 2 loading on the Sohar-Tamatave route, they said.
-- Sameer Mohindru, Ellie Lin
Platts East of Suez Dirty Tanker Daily Commentary
Gulf of Oman VLCC rates fall below w600
Suezmax, Aframax markets face further downward pressure
The East of Suez VLCC market weakened further Oct. 23, with rates for Gulf of Oman loadings slipping below w600. However, the speed and scale of the decline prompted market participants to question whether similarly steep falls could continue.
"I don't think rates will fall much further. There may be some further declines, but they are unlikely to be as fast or as steep," said a VLCC owner.
Another VLCC owner said the rate would depend on individual owners, with some willing to follow rates lower while others could resist.
Cargoes were heard to have been covered quietly, with AMCL and COSCO ships reportedly put on subjects by a Persian Gulf supplier and Shell for the loading window of end-October to Nov. 5 at w550-575.
The latest reported fixture was at w575, while some market participants believed w550 could not readily be repeated.
Meanwhile, owners loading from Yanbu were heard to have secured rates above the last reported level.
Fixture-wise, Atokos was heard to be put on subjects by Pertamina to load 270,000 mt crude from Yanbu to Cilacap Oct. 30 at a lumpsum of $52.5 million.
Market sources also said four Middle East-loading cargoes were expected to enter the market Oct. 23.
Market sources said reports of further attacks this week along the passage from inside the Persian Gulf to the Gulf of Oman had reinforced the risk premium for inside-Gulf loadings, helping rates hold up better than those for Gulf of Oman loadings.
The Suezmax market was expected by market sources to come under further pressure as VLCC rates continued to decline.
Aframax rates also edged lower amid a slowdown in cargo inquiries this week.
Fresh cargoes emerged Oct. 23, with Vietsea heard seeking an Aframax for loading of 80,000 mt of vacuum gasoil Nov. 3 from Singapore to Dung Quat, while Vitol was heard seeking an Aframax for loading Nov. 6 from Kimanis to Geelong.
Australian loading rates also remained under pressure. "Charterers won't be particularly keen to take Aframaxes for condensate at the moment as LR2s are cheaper," a broker said.

The West of Suez dirty tanker market saw VLCC rates out of West Africa fall Oct. 23, though sentiment began to steady as previously unreported fixtures came to light. Meanwhile, Suezmax rates in both West Africa and the Black Sea pushed higher on firm fundamentals.

In fresh business, HOB was heard to have booked the VL Bright for a 270,000 mt voyage from Sidi Kerir to South Korea for a Nov. 27 laycan, although the rate was not disclosed.
In the West African Suezmax market, much of the business was conducted privately, with few public cargo offerings and charterers showing little urgency to secure vessels.

News
Poland's Orlen says refinery throughput dips 1% in Q3
Wholesale fuel sales drop 5% year-over-year
Upstream output falls 8% to 4.28 million boe
Company books $410M impairment charges in Q3

Poland's largest refiner Orlen SA said late Oct. 22 its third quarter throughput was down 1% year-over-year to 10.1 million metric tons (about 801,400 barrels per day) and utilization of its refineries was stable at 94%.
Wholesale downstream fuel sales to external customers in the July-September period were down 5% year-over-year to just under 8 million mt, Orlen said in market filing of selected Q3 operating results.
While Orlen's refining capacity utilization in Q3 was unchanged year on year, it increased by around 8% from the second quarter. The company's CFO Slawomir Jdrzejczyk said in August that maintenance overhauls in Poland and the Czech Republic had lowered throughput and utilization in the April-June quarter.
Retail fuel sales to external customers fell 2% to 2.94 million mt.
In the upstream segment, Orlen said its crude oil, condensate and NGLs output fell 8% year-over-year to 4.28 million barrels of oil equivalent. Orlen's CFO said in August the company was planning maintenance shutdowns in the upstream segment in Q3.
Orlen said the figures are estimates and may be revised in the Q3 results report to be published on Nov. 5.
The company said it will recognize impairment charges totaling 1.6 billion zlotys ($410 million) in its Q3 financial report thanks to expenditures related to the New Chemistry petrochemicals project launched in 2021.
-- Adam Easton
Mexico announces shift in Pemex investment focus amid restructuring, funding concerns
Pemex allocates 80% of funds to exploration
Debt reduction relies on government transfers
Observers question the program's financial viability
Mexico's state oil company Pemex plans to allocate nearly 80% of its proposed Peso 1.85 trillion ($107 billion) investment through 2030 to exploration and production, underscoring its upstream focus amid financing concerns and workforce reductions.
Pemex said Oct. 21 that exploration and production would receive Peso 1.464 trillion under its 2025-2030 program, compared with Peso 185.6 billion for refining, Peso 104.3 billion for logistics, Peso 71 billion for petrochemicals and fertilizers, and Peso 21 billion for gas processing.
Pemex CEO Juan Carlos Carpio Fragoso said the program would be financed using the company's own resources, although he did not provide detailed cash flow projections or specify how individual projects would be funded.
The announcement came without much fanfare, but the allocation is seen by many as the only way the government could sustain Mexico's oil and gas production after years of substantial investment in refining infrastructure, including the Olmeca refinery at Dos Bocas and the acquisition of Deer Park in Texas.
"Pemex urgently needs that investment," said Ricardo González, legal Mexico representative at Capricorn Energy.

© 2026 by S&P Global Inc. All rights reserved.

35

©2026 S&P Global Inc. Any unauthorized use of this copyrighted material constitutes infringement of our intellectual property and will subject you to possible legal action.

Platts Oilgram Price Report

October 23, 2026

"If it doesn't, it will end up without enough crude to process," González said.
The emphasis on upstream development builds on Pemex's February 2025 work plan, which already contemplated substantial investment in exploration, drilling and field development.
However, the announcement has renewed questions about the company's ability to finance its ambitions, particularly given its continued dependence on government support.
Observers question the program's financial viability, arguing that government transfers, tax reductions and additional borrowing could shift the financial burden rather than resolve Pemex's underlying cash constraints.
"Reliance on funds guaranteed by the government, dependent on development banks like Banobras, represents a systemic risk, disguised as institutional financing," said Gabriel Becerra Dingler, an independent energy analyst and partner at Solmex Energía, a clean energy solutions company.
Betting on complementary private investments requires an environment of trust, rule of law and attractive returns, which the government has systematically eroded with its recent decisions, he said.
Pemex's recent reduction in debt has depended on federal assistance rather than internally generated cash surpluses, with free cash flow remaining negative after investment, observers say.
The Mexican Institute for Competitiveness estimates that Pemex received approximately Peso 1.88 trillion in federal support between January 2019 and June 2026, including capital contributions, tax incentives and extraordinary assistance.
The amount is roughly equivalent to the investment program now proposed, although the figures cover different periods and purposes.
Yet, those resources have done little to solve the situation the state oil and gas company faces. Pemex reported that its financial debt had declined 21% from the end of 2024 to $77.5 billion in June 2026, an improvement management said would reduce interest expenses and free resources for productive investment. The debt to its contractors remains elevated, estimated at roughly $20 billion by the first semester of 2026.
Workforce restructuring The investment program coincides with efforts to reduce
personnel costs and streamline Pemex's organizational structure. Mexican media have reported that dismissals and salary-
grade reductions are beginning to affect employees across several areas of the company, citing representatives of the National Union of Petroleum Technicians and Professionals.
The organization reported dismissals in Reynosa, Tamaulipas, and reductions in salary grades for some employees who remained with the company. The total number of affected workers has not been established.
The simultaneous pursuit of greater upstream investment and a smaller workforce raises questions about Pemex's ability to retain the technical and managerial expertise required for drilling and field development, according to observers.
-- Sheky Espejo

OIL FUTURES: ICE Brent highest since April on Middle East supply risksICE December Brent tops $116/b ...from page 1
Observed and assumed vessel movements through the Strait of Hormuz increased to 23 on Oct. 22, up from a revised 19 transits recorded on Oct. 21, CAS data showed, while transits through the Bab al-Mandab Strait declined to 27 from 35 a day earlier.
"The threat is no longer one route or one front: Iran, the Houthis in Yemen and militias in Iraq can all put Middle East oil infrastructure at risk," according to the latest crude oil market outlook from S&P Global CERA.
Saudi crude continues to reach international markets, including via Yanbu, the Suez Canal and Egypt's SUMED pipeline, but the latest attacks have put the market on edge over both production infrastructure and alternative export routes out of the Middle East.
"The rally is being driven less by current fundamentals than by mounting supply concerns," Patterson and Manthey said. "Even if tensions don't re-escalate, the mere possibility is likely to keep a sizeable geopolitical risk premium embedded in prices."

REFINERY NEWS: India's BPCL throughput up 6% in Q2
Refinery: Three
Owner: Bharat Petroleum Corp Ltd.
Overall capacity: 35.3 million mt (706,000 b/d)
India's No.2 state-run refiner Bharat Petroleum Corp. Ltd. processed 10.41 million mt (826,000 b/d) in the July-September period, or the second quarter, company officials said on Oct. 23, reflecting 6% year-over-year rise in processing despite the Middle East conflict.
In Q2, BPCL refined throughput 2.6% higher than in the previous quarter (January-March), they said.
In Q2, BPCL sold 13.47 million mt of refined products, up 6.3% from a year ago and down 1.1% from the previous quarter, they said.
In Q2, BPCL exported 590,000 mt of refined products, up 63.9% a year ago and 15.7% from the January-March quarter, the company said in a regulatory filing.
BPCL operates three refineries: Mumbai on the west coast, Kochi in south India and Bina in central India, according to its website.
BPCL's fiscal year runs from April to March, the company said.
For the April-September period, or H1, BPCL processed 20.56 million mt, up 1.6% year over year, they said.
In H1, BPCL's sales stood at 27 million mt, up 3.2% year over year, they said.

REFINERY NEWS: Kazakhstan's Shymkent expansion reports progress
Refinery: Shymkent, Kazakhstan
Owner: PetroKazakhstan Oil Products LLP
Overall capacity: 6 mil mt/year (120,000 b/d)
The development of the feasibility study for the expansion of Kazakhstan's Shymkent refinery is nearing completion, with the preparations for the front-end engineering and design (FEED) stage underway, KazMunayGaz said Oct. 22 following a meeting with China's CNPC.
The refinery's capacity is set to be expanded from 6 million metric tons/year now to 12 million mt/year in 2030, KMG said in a statement.
During the meeting both sides discussed the expansion of the Kenkiyak-Atyrau and Kenkiyak-Kumkol pipelines' capacity to a respective 15 million mt/year and 20 million mt/year in order to secure crude oil to the Shymkent refinery. KMG and CNPC have decided to work on developing the respective project documentation.
Kazakhstan has previously indicated plans to expand the two pipelines from Kenkiyak to Atyrau from 6 million mt/year to 15 million mt/year and from Kenkiyak to Kumkol from 10 million mt/ year to 20 million mt/year.
Shymkent is 50% owned by CNPC and 50% by KMG. -- Elza Turner, Vladislav Vorotnikov
REFINERY NEWS: Israel's Bazan summoned for hearing on permit renewal
Refinery: Haifa, Israel
Owner: Bazan Group (formerly Oil Refineries, ORL)
Overall capacity: 9.8 million mt/year (about 197,000 b/d)
Israel's Bazan Group said Oct. 22 that it has been summoned by the Haifa Municipality to a hearing regarding the renewal of permits for its operations.
The hearing, which will take place Oct. 25, is being held "against the background of various claims" by the municipality regarding environmental issues.
Local media have reported that, following unusually high benzene pollution in the area of the plant earlier in October, the municipality was considering not renewing the permits, which are due to expire at the end of October.

Bazan said Oct. 22 that the municipality had noted that "the outcome of the hearing may be the issuance of an administrative closure order or an application to the court for a judicial closure order and non-renewal of the temporary permits."
The company said that if the license is not renewed, "it may have a material adverse effect" on its operations.
However, it also said that "all authorized approving parties have given their approval for the renewal of the temporary permits."
On Oct. 12, Bazan said in a statement that during periodic maintenance of its aromatics facilities, "high benzene concentrations were measured at several monitoring points in the area."
Work had been suspended and actions were being taken "to resume the work in coordination with the Ministry of Environmental Protection," it added.
Bazan has previously said it would carry out planned maintenance during the fourth quarter of 2026, including works on the continuous catalytic reformer, isomerization unit, aromatics facilities and ancillary facilities.
-- Elza Turner

Subscriber Notes

Throughput at Eni S.p.A.'s Italian refineries rose 9% year over year to 4.14 million metric tons in the third quarter of 2026, the company said Oct. 23.
In the first nine months of 2026, throughput was down 5% at 10.28 million mt.
The Q3 increase was attributed to "higher volumes at the Taranto refinery due to higher plant availability."
Eni's Q3 throughput in other refineries globally was down 12% to 2.47 million mt "due to lower volumes processed connected to the closure of the Strait of Hormuz." Throughput was also 19% down at 6.43 million mt in January-September.
The company reported an average utilization rate of 86% across its refineries in Q3, up from 84% in Q3 2025 and 76% in the nine months of 2026, down from 81% in the same year-ago period.
Eni's benchmark refining margin in Q3 was $28.1/b, up from $8.9/b a year earlier, "mainly due to more favorable middle distillate crack spreads reflecting the continuing product tightness in connection with disrupted flows from the Middle East and plant outages against a backdrop of refinery closures in the Atlantic Basin."
In January-September, the margin was $15.5/b, up from $5.8/b. Bio throughput in Q3 was 262,000 mt, down from 315,000 mt in the year-ago quarter, "driven by lower volumes processed at the Gela and Venice biorefineries following the planned shutdown, in order to enhance biorefinery configuration, partially offset by better performance of Chalmette biorefinery." In January-September, bio throughput was 789,000 mt, down from 881,000 mt, "mainly reflecting the shutdown at Venice." Average utilization was 71% in Q3, down from 85% year over year, and 70% in January-September, down from 79% in the same year-ago period.
-- Elza Turner
REFINERY NEWS: Italy's Augusta up and running amid protests
Refinery: Augusta, Sicily, Italy
Owner: Sonatrach
Overall capacity: 160,000 b/d

Platts to launch forward derivative assessments for STS Lome gasoline, diesel and jet fuel
Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, has noted increasing demand for transparency on West African refined products, both on a spot market basis and for forward delivery. In response, Platts will start publishing daily forward swap assessments for the existing Gasoline STS Lome, Low Sulfur Diesel STS Lome, and Jet Fuel STS Lome physical price assessments, effective Nov. 30, 2026. These will be published as Month 1, Month 2, and Month 3 assessments.
In order to assess the derivatives on an editorial basis, Platts will consult a range of market information, including information heard in the physical market, relevant forward exchange-traded data, as well as related markets.
The published assessments will correspond with delivery dates three months forward, starting with the front month [M1], followed by M2 and M3.

Platts to launch STS Lome Gasoline differentials to Eurobob FOB AR assessments
Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, will launch a new daily price assessment reflecting the differential between the Gasoline STS Lome assessment and the Gasoline Eurobob FOB AR barge assessment, effective Nov. 23, 2026.
This follows increased demand for transparency on West African gasoline differentials, as Nigeria's Dangote refinery continues to reshape flows and pricing dynamics in the Atlantic Basin.
The assessed differential is calculated as the difference between the physical Gasoline STS Lome assessment and the midpoint of the three-to-10-day forward strip, itself derived from the established Gasoline Eurobob FOB AR barge assessment derivative assessments.
The new assessments will provide a transparent indication of the value of STS Lome gasoline relative to Northwest European gasoline blendstock assessments, and will complement Platts existing outright STS Lome gasoline differentials against Platts Premium 10ppm FOB Mediterranean cargoes and Premium 10ppm FOB Rotterdam barges.

Platts to amend freight calculation for Oman, Murban CFR North Asia crude assessments Nov. 2
Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, will amend the freight calculation used in its daily Oman and Murban CFR North Asia crude assessments, effective Nov. 2, 2026.
Under the change, Platts will use the daily VLCC Gulf of Omanto-Far East freight assessment in $/mt (DOOEW00), which was launched Aug. 3, in its calculation of the outright price of Oman and Murban delivered into North Asia at 4:30 pm Singapore time (0830 GMT).
The change will better reflect the shipping economics for the two grades, with Murban primarily loading from Fujairah port and Oman from Mina Al Fahal port outside the Strait of Hormuz.
Currently, the freight calculation is based on the daily VLCC Persian Gulf-to-Far East freight assessment in $/mt (TDAAB00).
Platts first proposed the change in a subscriber note published Sept. 28, 2026.
The freight calculation will continue to use the conversion factor for metric tons to barrels, as applied to the grades, in line with their prevailing qualities. The conversion factors are currently 7.32 for Oman and 7.62 for Murban.
In line with this change, Platts will also use the daily VLCC Gulf of Oman-to-Far East worldscale freight assessment (DIOEW00) in the freight calculation for Murban in its Indonesian Minas, Cinta and Widuri crude market parity prices.
Under the current MPP methodology for Minas, Cinta and Widuri, Murban -- as one of five basket grades -- is normalized for delivery to Singapore, accounting for freight. This is currently calculated using the daily VLCC Persian Gulf-to-Singapore worldscale freight assessment (PFAOD00).
The affected assessments appear in Crude Oil Marketwire, Oilgram Price Report, LNG Daily, Platts OPR World Crude Table, Platts Global Alert/Platts Crude Oil Alert pages 1250, 1805, 2202, 2203, 2211, 2216, 2217, 2244, 2245, 2264, 2265, 2280, 2281, 2811 and 2812, LNG Alert pages 0050 and 0051, Platts Natural Gas Alert pages 1024 and 1069, and under the price database codes below:

Platts amends Abu Dhabi crude OSPs publication Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, has updated its publication
of Abu Dhabi crude official selling prices effective Oct. 8, 2026, in line with changes announced by Abu Dhabi National Oil Co. (ADNOC).
In July, ADNOC said it would adopt a prompt-month pricing methodology based on the Platts Dubai benchmark (PCAAT00) plus an ADNOC-announced differential for the OSPs of its Abu Dhabi onshore and offshore crude grades, with effect from November 2026 loadings. ADNOC added that it would announce the OSP differentials for Murban, Das, Umm Lulu and Upper Zakum in the month prior to the delivery month.
Accordingly, Platts will publish the monthly OSP differentials for the four grades, basis Platts Dubai, beginning with the November differentials issued by ADNOC on Oct. 8, 2026.
Platts first announced the change in a subscriber note published on Aug. 31, 2026.
The OSP differentials are published under the following reinstated codes:

Grade Murban Umm Lulu Das Blend Upper Zakum

OSP Diff to Dubai AAIOS00 OSLUD00 AAXPL00 AAIOV00

Platts previously published the OSP differentials under these codes until May 2021, when they were then suspended due to a change in the ADNOC pricing methodology.
From November, the existing codes for the outright OSPs for Murban (AAIKZ00), Upper Zakum (AAILC00), Das Blend (AAXPH00) and Umm Lulu (OSLUF00) will remain unchanged. Platts will publish the outright OSPs of the four grades following the publication of the average of Platts Dubai crude assessments during the month of loading.
In view of the changes, Platts is no longer updating the codes reflecting Upper Zakum, Das Blend and Umm Lulu OSP differentials against Murban, as well as the codes for all four grades, including Murban, calculated against the monthly average of Platts cash Dubai 1st Line assessment in the month of trade.
The following codes will no longer be updated from October:

Grade Murban Umm Lulu Das Blend Upper Zakum

Diff to Dubai AOSPA00 AOSPB00 AOSPC00 AOSPD00

Diff to Murban OSP
AOSPE00 AOSPF00 AOSPG00

ADNOC's OSPs are published in the Platts price database and on Platts Global Alert page 1051.

Platts to launch daily FOB Middle East bitumen assessments from Nov. 2
Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, will launch daily VG30 and VG40 bitumen spot assessments for FOB Middle East, effective Nov. 2, 2026.
The new assessments will complement Platts' existing daily and weekly bitumen assessments, providing greater price transparency and information in the waterborne trade for the product in the Asia-Pacific and Middle East markets.
Platts will launch two new daily outright assessments and their respective monthly averages. These assessments will reflect bulk viscosity grade 30 and VG40 grade bitumen, as per IS 73:2013 specifications, loading 10-30 days forward from the date of publication in the Middle East.

Pricing information for material with other specifications, such as PEN 60/70, may be considered for the assessments, normalized to the proposed standard.
The assessments will reflect market value at the close of Asian trading at 4:30 pm Singapore time and follow the Singapore publication schedule. Bids, offers and trades reported throughout the day may be considered in the assessment process, with values normalized to the market close at 4:30 pm Singapore time.

Platts proposes to amend rollover date of Asian Dated Brent Strip at London close
Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, is proposing to amend the rollover date of its Asian Dated Brent Strip at London close assessment to the first publication day of the month, from Dec. 1, 2026.
The proposed change, which follows Platts review of historical data sets, would align the ADB Strip at London close assessment with the current rollover date of its ADB Strip at Asia close assessment.
Currently, this assessment, which reflects the forward value of Dated Brent two months ahead of the date of publication at the 4:30 pm London close, rolls over on the 9th of the month.
The assessments affected by this change appear in Crude Oil Marketwire, Asia Pacific/Arab Gulf Marketscan, Oilgram Price Report, Platts Global Alert/ Platts Crude Oil Alert pages 2206, 2213, 2215, 2217, 2219, 2260, 2261, 2262, 2263, 2264, 2265, 2266, 2267, 2268, 2269, 2270, 2271, 2280, 2281, 2558, 2559, and under their price database codes as below:

Platts revises measurement units, descriptions for FO Licht ethanol prices

Cubic Meter Pure Alcohol (CPA) · Type: Volume Unit · Definition: A unit of volume that measures the volume of pure (100%) alcohol. One CBMPA equals 1 cubic meter (1,000 liters) of pure alcohol. Unlike a standard cubic meter (m³), which measures the total volume of any substance (liquid, gas, or solid), CBMPA refers specifically to the ethanol content, not the total liquid volume.

Units of measurements and descriptions updated for Platts FOLicht ethanol prices The following Platts FOLicht ethanol prices have had their Units of measurements and descriptions updated, as summarized below. Hectoliter Pure Alcohol (HPA) · Type: Volume Unit · Definition: A unit of volume that measures the volume of pure (100%) alcohol. One HLTPA equals 1 hectoliter (100 liters) of pure alcohol. Unlike a standard hectoliter (hL), which measures the total volume of any liquid, HLTPA refers only to the pure alcohol content, regardless of the concentration in the original beverage.

Platts proposes to launch weighted indexes for Chicago CBOB, ULSD
Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, is proposing to launch Chicago basket indexes for gasoline and diesel from March 2, incorporating the independent assessments for the major pipelines and Buckeye Complex and weighting them by trading volume, as well as adding a new, separate assessment for Chicago West Shore and Badger pipeline ULSD.
Feedback from market participants has indicated the need for Chicago-weighted basket indexes that reflect recent volumes traded on the Badger, West Shore, and Wolverine pipelines, as well as the Buckeye Complex.
The proposed weightings would be calculated by tracking recent trades across the West Shore, Badger, and Wolverine pipelines, as well as the Buckeye Complex. Total reported trades would be compiled on a cyclical basis, assuming a minimum volume of 10,000 barrels where not specified. These trades will then be sorted by pipeline to determine the percentage contribution of each component.
The weighted index would be calculated from the average of the differentials to NYMEX futures for the West Shore/Badger pipelines, Wolverine pipeline and BCX assessments, adjusted by their corresponding weightings.
For example, if Platts is assessing Cycle 2 for Chicago ULSD and traded volumes from the previous cycles came out to

20% West Shore and Badger, 30% Wolverine and 50% Buckeye Complex and the pipeline differentials were assessed at plus 2 cents/gal, plus 3 cents/gal and plus 3.50 cents/gal, respectively, the index basket differential would be plus 3.05 cents/gal.
Platts' current assessments for Chicago CBOB and ULSD are as follows:

Symbol
AATHA00 AGCWC00 ACBAE00 AAREL00 ACBAA00 AGCWA00

Name
ULSD Chicago pipeline ULSD Chicago Wolverine Pipeline ULSD Chicago BCX Gasoline CBOB Chicago pipeline Gasoline CBOB Chicago BCX Gasoline CBOB Chicago Wolverine Pipeline

The proposed Chicago basket indexes would be as follows:
Name
Gasoline CBOB Chicago Basket ULSD Chicago Basket

Platts is concurrently seeking feedback on how to incorporate West Shore and Badger into the weighted index when the 1 psi waiver is in place during the summer. More information on how Platts will adjust the West Shore and Badger assessments in accordance with the 1 psi waiver can be found here.
Additionally, Platts seeks feedback on the naming conventions for the proposal. Platts proposes to amend the assessment name from Gasoline CBOB Chicago pipeline to Gasoline CBOB West Shore and Badger pipeline to better represent the products assessed. This change follows the Feb. 3, 2025, removal of the Wolverine Pipeline from the basket underpinning the generic Chicago assessment due to the anticipated removal of the 1 psi waiver in multiple Midwest states and a division in RVP.
Platts is also proposing to launch a separate West Shore/ Badger assessment for Chicago ULSD; this would not replace the existing generic assessment, which would remain unchanged. The name of the new assessment would be ULSD Chicago West Shore and Badger Pipeline.
The indexes would appear on PGA pages 350, 351, 154, 24, 482, 410 and 411, as well as in US Marketscan, Oilgram Price Report, and on Platts Connect.
Please send all feedback and comments by Jan. 23 to americas_products@spglobal.com and pricegroup@spglobal.com.
For written comments, please provide a clear indication if comments are not intended for publication by Platts for public viewing. Platts will consider all comments received and will make comments not marked as confidential available upon request.
Platts invites feedback on US gasoline RVP calendar for 2026 As part of its commitment to open and transparent pricing
and product specifications, Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, would like to invite feedback regarding publishing dates and/or cycles for 2026 US gasoline RVP shifts.
The 2026 calendar largely follows 2025 norms, with no notable deviations from prior years. The calendar will be updated throughout the year as more information becomes available.

© 2026 by S&P Global Inc. All rights reserved.

46

©2026 S&P Global Inc. Any unauthorized use of this copyrighted material constitutes infringement of our intellectual property and will subject you to possible legal action.

Platts Oilgram Price Report

October 23, 2026

The shift dates are based on relevant pipeline schedules, historical information, and feedback from market participants.
US Gulf Coast shifts are based on the Colonial Pipeline schedule. US Atlantic Coast shifts are based on Buckeye Pipeline and Colonial Pipeline calendars.
On the West Coast, Los Angeles, San Francisco and Arizona RVP changes are based on Kinder Morgan's SFPP schedule, while Portland and Seattle shifts are based on Olympic Pipeline's calendar.
In the Midwest, Group 3 shifts are based on the ONEOK Pipeline schedule, while Chicago shifts are based on Explorer Pipeline and Buckeye Complex schedules.

The US Environmental Protection Agency removed a waiver in eight Midwestern states that allowed for an additional 1 psi during the summer. Platts will adjust the RVP Calendar for Chicago CBOB in accordance with the implementation of the waiver. More information can be found here.
For questions or comments, please contact americas_products@spglobal.com with a cc to pricegroup@spglobal.com.
For written comments, please provide a clear indication if comments are not intended for publication by Platts for public viewing. Platts will consider all comments received and will make comments not marked as confidential available upon request.

2-DAY TRAINING
Upstream Fundamentals and Economics
June 2-3 | Houston

Fossil fuels dominate the energy mix representing over 80% of global energy demand. With geopolitical tensions influencing supply volumes and lower price, the S&P Global Commodity Insights "Upstream Fundamentals and Economics" course will equip you with the insights and strategies needed to navigate a constantly changing environment, meet challenges with informed decisions, and drive business growth.
Our prestigious Upstream Fundamentals and Economics Training is a great opportunity to hear from a range of S&P Global experts sharing their expertise, regional trends and industry insights, as well as concrete case studies and analyses.
WHO SHOULD ATTEND
This course would be suitable for:
· Technical and non-technical professionals, with experience or new recruits, from crude oil and refined products companies
· Governments, EPCs, OEMs, Suppliers, Investors · Personnel from Projects, Planning, Business Development,
Commercial

Judson Jacobs Executive Director, S&P Global Energy
Stephanie Vargas Ortega Technical Research Principal, Vantage LATAM & FNA Team Lead S&P Global Energy

Siddhartha Sen Director, Costs and Supply Chain, S&P Global Energy
